## Gauntlet (spelljammer)

The Gauntlet was a refitted scorpion that served as flagship of the Gauntlet, the self-proclaimed police force of the Tears of Selûne in the mid‒14 century DR.

Description
All the Gauntlet'''s  weapons had been magically enhanced for increased accuracy. The catapult mounted on the ship's tail was mounted on a turret that allowed it to freely rotate in all directions. The catapult on the main deck also had a similar turret, which allowed it to rotate almost entirely freely, except in directions that would result in it hitting the ship's tail.

History
The Gauntlet'' was purchased from the arcane by the paladin Kyriel Alathar Pellinore after he left his position in the Council of Elders of Everlund to personally pursue justice against an illithid attack that captured 200 people, including his family.

The ship was involved in the organization's rise to fame when it attacked and dismantled a neogi slave trade cell that operated on one of the asteroids of the Tears. The attack destroyed all buildings on the asteroid and killed nearly all of the 100 neogi and several mind flayers who were also trading altered humanoids from their farms on Glyth. The few surviving slavers reported the events of the attack to other members of their species, which resulted in a bounty of more than 100,000 gp for the capture of the ship.

Inhabitants
The ship's captain was Kyriel, who was assisted by his apprentice Ansalok. Three mages took turns at the spelljamming helm: Cambrigha, Savion, and Vom. Joining the crew were also three priests: Liasa, Sunea, and Kierie. The three mages had six apprentices who were also part of the crew: Alekra, Vicales, Nogard, Sarden, Chare, and Sharondel.

## Galea

Galea was an elven paladin and adventurer on the Sword Coast.

Personality
Galea's motivation for adventuring was to better the world and to gain prestige. She believed that joy and love were precious things that should be fostered in an often too-dark world, and had taken the paladin Oath of the Ancients in this pursuit. She was fond of art, music, beauty, and merrymaking, because she felt these things could engender hope, life, and light during dark times.

Relationships
She was part of an adventuring troupe with the tiefling warlock Prosper, the human wizard Sefris, and the dragonborn barbarian Vrondiss.

## Gauntlets of Faerglamer

Gauntlets of Faerglamer were enchanted gauntlets first created by Bilnur Faerglamer.

Description
Depending on the form their wearer last commanded, the Gauntlets could be either lace-black-purple-"glittershard" elbow-length gauzy silk ladies' gloves, dirty, poorly wrapped bandages, or sleek and massive yet silent war-gauntlets in perfect condition.

Powers
Besides being able to shift between forms, gauntlets of Faerglamer were invariably supple enough for work of any delicacy, and made it impossible to drop objects by accident. So long as they were worn by a being of approximately human size, and worn on arms, they worked. They communicated all their powers to the wearer's mind: they automatically destroyed any of the Bigby's Hand spells at a touch, glow with faerie fire as willed, and cause up to a ton of anything to fall slowly, as per feather fall. Any two of those powers could be active at once, regardless of other powers being active.

Any left glove could reflect magic missiles back to their source, and every hour, the wearer could confer invisibility upon themselves or those they touched for up to 12 minutes. Once every 3 hours and 20 minutes, the left gauntlet could fire off a thrust that flew for  at any target unerringly, dealing rather severe damage.

Right gauntlets reflected instead lightning bolt and chain lightning spells back at their source; the wearer could use dimension door to move up to  every 40 minutes, and fire off a forcethrust effect every three hours and twenty minutes. This effect was a  wide and  long ruby ray that spoiled the spellcasting of any creature it touched, also dealing some damage. Its damage was doubled against any non-gaseous undead; all targets were immobilized for a minute due to the magic.

Gauntlets that were enchanted together, as a matched pair, granted additional powers when worn together, above and beyond those of the left and right gauntlet separately. Any creature touched with matched gauntlets could be affected as per hold monster, lasting for four minutes; it would not work again on the same target until four hours had passed. They also granted water breathing, allowed the user to employ locate object for up to 10 minutes every 12 days, a 2-hour passwall every 22 days, and a delayed blast fireball at 80% strength with a range of  every 6 days.

Beyond its base powers, only one such power could be active at a time.

History
Bilnur Faerglamer made hundreds of such gauntlets over his career and shipped them out through a merchant coster that served as a middleman, before becoming the Magister; after he became the Magister, he continued, but now hiding spell scrolls inside the gauntlets he crafted. many pairs survived until the late 14 century, showing up in unexpected places.

Rumors & Legends
Special matched pairs were rumored to have more powers that only awakened in very specific situations.

## Garshond

Garshond was a priest of Helm active during the mid–13 century DR, and one of the most prominent Helmite priests of his generation.

Possessions
Helm of Helm
Garshond was the the first known owner of the Helm of Helm, a blessed helmet and one of the church of Helm's holy books.

History
Garshond was active near Iriaebor during the, defending the region against orc and goblinoid armies. During one of those battles, a holy helmet rose out of a battlefield bog and presented itself to the cleric. It was unclear whether Helm created the item at that moment, or took it from the bones of an older champion of his faith laying under the waters of the bog.

Garshond took the relic back to the Watchtower of the Vigilant, his temple in Harkstag. There he organized a Council of Helm by inviting representatives from important families of Iriaebor, Waterdeep and Scornubel. This successful reunion led to the creation of the Vigilant Riders during the, and established Garshond as one of the most prominent Helmite priests of his generation.

Garshond took part of many fights alongside the Riders, defending the region against brigands and goblinkin during what would later be known as Garshond's War. He lost his life in one such battles, during the .

## Gargoyle cloak

A gargoyle cloak was an enchanted cloak capable of transforming the wearer into a gargoyle.

Powers
The wearer could use the cloak's power up to three times per day. The transformation occurred as soon as the cloak was donned. There was a cumulative risk that the wearer would actually believe they were a gargoyle the longer they remained in that form. If this occurred, the wearer could only be returned to their normal form via a wish.

Notable Owners
Sememmon, the ruler of Darkhold, owned a gargoyle cloak.

## Garlen

Garlen was a young man from Bloodstone Village in Damara who helped liberate his home from the bandit army of Vaasa in the mid–14 century DR.

Description
At age 19, he was  tall, weighed, and had brown hair and eyes.

Personality
Garlen and his cousin Garvin were both very excitable and prone to hero worship. They often finished each other's sentences.

Possessions
Garlen carried a short sword and shortbow.

Relationships
Garlen was close with his cousin, Garvin.

History
In the, Garlen and his cousin Garvin left Bloodstone Village and traveled to the town of Valls to find heroes who could deliver their hometown from Vaasa's oppression. While attending the Circus of Doctor Trundles, the boys witnessed Gareth Dragonsbane and his adventuring companions put down an outbreak of rampaging monsters. impressed by their skill and bravery, the boys asked the heroes for their aid, and then guided them through the Galena Mountains to Bloodstone. Once there, Garlen and Garvin arranged a meeting between the adventurers and the town's leader, Baron Tranth.

## Garments of elvenkind

Garments of elvenkind was a spell used by elven clerics.

Effects
This spell allowed the caster to enchant a pair of boots, a cloak, and a pair of bracers. Once enchanted, the cloak acted as a cloak of elvenkind, the boots as boots of elvenkind, and the bracers as bracers of archery. The enchanted clothing could be used by any creature but they must use all three items, otherwise the spell failed. The spell was vulnerable to dispel magic. Once the spell expired, the items returned to their normal state.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required several material components. Along with the caster's holy symbol, the spell required three drops from a potion of invisibility, three small items that were once enchanted with a silence dweomer, and three enchanted arrows. Every material component except the holy symbol was consumed by the spell.

## Galast

Galast was a Malarite druid active in the High Forest during the middle of the 14 century DR. He was usually followed by the wolf Fang, his animal companion.

History
Galast was born from a couple of outlaws living in the High Forest. Raised in the faith of Malar, he became a tracker and a scout, hunting to survive and hiding to escape from the bounty hunters tracking his parents.

After witnessing an elder druid known as "Wolf Dragon" single-handedly destroy an elven warband, Galast became his apprentice and learnt druidcraft. Thrice did his new mentor try to give him the gift of lycanthropy, thrice did he fail: Galast displayed some changes, but never fully succumbed to the curse. Disgusted, Wolf Dragon sent his pupil away to wander the forest on his own.

While roaming the forest, Galast found a lost spellbook in a bear cave. Unaware that the "Earthmother's Weapons" was a Chauntean holy book, Galast deciphered its content and learned the spells it contained. Despite being spells from his sworn enemies, Malar deemed them useful in the hands of his worshiper, and granted their power to the druid. By the, Galast was still the owner of this sacred tome.

## Garth Street

Garth Street was a street in Suzail, the capital of Cormyr, that stretched from the Promenade to the city's open-air plaza Market.

Description
The street began at the northernmost point of the Market from where it sharply moved north and bowed to the northeast past Doloph Street and the Hunt Bide, ending at the Promenade.

Notable Locations
Hardshutters House, the guildhall of the Roofers, Thatchers, and Glaziers Guild that stood on the western side of Garth Street, where it began at the Marketplace.
The Crying Witch tavern that faced the Market, on the eastern side of Garth Street.
The Golden Goblin, a rowdy tavern on the eastern side, at the northern end of the first block on Garth Street.
Culder Darshoon's Cobbler Shop, a humble cobbler store ran by Culder Darshoon in the 14 century DR, that stood one door west off the intersection with the Hunt Bide.
Tathlin's Discreet Errands, a message runners' business on the intersection of the Hunt Bide and Garth Street.
The Leaning Post, a quiet inn owned by the Hawklin noble family of Cormyr, that stood on the northern side of Garth Street where it crossed the Hunt Bide.
The Gilded Feather, the most expensive pretty-parlor in Suzail that stood on Garth Street's eastern side on the intersection with the Promenade.

## Galan Goadulphyn

Galan Goadulphyn, also Galan the Gallant, was an inhabitant of Myth Drannor in the 3 century DR.

Appearance
He had only one eye. After falling off a sheer rock face, he became disfigured to the point of being nearly unrecognizable as an elf.

Abilities
He was an excellent throw with the knife.

History
Galan was a warrior of Cormanthor. During, he was heavily indebted to a number of Houses in Cormanthyr; while pondering how to acquire different loans to pay off his creditors, he learned of Elminster Aumar being named armathor from Athtar Nlossae; while at first violently skeptical of the importance of such things, he became interested in the idea of using the chaos as cover to eliminate some rivals. Not long after, they nearly became involved in the duel between Elminster and Delmuth Echorn, as the spell duel threatened to bury or blast a cache of coins of Galan's. Delmuth Echorn's skull, which came loose in the battle, was launched afterwards into their midst as they rushed to the cache, slaying his companion and throwing Galan off a cliff.

Later, while wandering the forest in hunger, he came across a mushroom farmer; he was so hungry that he reached for the mushrooms before realizing they belonged to someone. The farmer briefly tried to fight but found himself far overmatched, then mistook mistook him for the new human armathor, and worried about offense, offered him all he carried. After seeing him slay a stirge without even looking, the farmer became convinced of his puissance, and yielded his packed lunch. Though puzzled, he was too hungry to refuse.

He returned to the city in  carrying a wealth of stolen dwarven gems in his boots; he was mistaken for a human by a patrol on the road, though he tried not to get spotted. Learning humans and dwarves were allowed in the city vexed him somewhat, but he still gave away a ruby to the armathor in charge, mostly as mockery- regretting the loss of a ruby almost instantly. Later he saw many elves flying under the power of the mythal; he thought to attempt to burgle a home or two while they were distracted, but he was stopped by Symrustar Auglamyr, who kissed him and enspelled all his gems to fly off his boots.

At the time of the laying of the mythal, he was the last of his line and used to disappear for months on end. However, by, he had married a human woman who bore him a son, Thallan Goadulphyn. He had since set himself to the task of restoring his house.

## Gaj

Gaj was a market town mostly inhabited by hard-working dwarves and gnomes, located to the southwest from the Blade Kingdoms, on the westernmost foothills of the Maerthwatch in the Vilhon Reach.

Description
As of the 14 century DR, Gaj was an unwalled settlement that housed numerous crafters, and areas surrounding it were farmed land.

Government
The town was ruled by an elected council of twelve called the Luth. The council members all were senior crafters. The Luth commanded the town's Gajmar, the title given to the leader of Gaj's town watch. The watch consisted of cudgel-armed "trusties" and spies who kept an eye on visitors and outsiders, as well as spellcasters who watched over outsider mages, adventurers, and other notable powerful individuals. The town watch prevented gangs and criminal organizations from forming in Gaj and stopped thefts, blackmail, and arson.

Trade
Being a major trade settlement, Gaj's industry was based around its shallow copper and silver mines with an occasional gold vein. The dwarven inhabitants worked the mines while gnomes manned the town's smelters and foundries. Gaj produced copper wire grips for tolls and weapons, wire jewelry mounts and handles, teapot handles, and other such items. These products were exported to coastal Chondath and Chessenta. In turn, Gaj imported clothing, woven textiles, and fine wine.

The town was fed by its farms that produced goat cheese, milk, mutton, and vegetables. Gaj also produced lower-quality wines and ales, both sour-tasting. The town's cuisine consisted of a variety of soups and stews with leeks, marrows, dilvarrens, roast goat, goat milk, and redroot. Gaj's specialty breads were barley breads and nutbreads, with walnuts—nicknamed "brains"—being prevalent.

Notable Inhabitants
 Lhlarauna Dashard Umurr, the retired adventurer and the Gajmar circa the .

Appendix
References

## Gargajaws

Gargajaws was a powerful, destructive spell capable of causing incredible damage to structures.

Effects
Upon casting the spell, a column of white flame rose from the caster's cupped palms for . The white flame split in three past that point, each flame forming into a shape akin to the clamlike, eyeless head of a beast; these jaws would then attack a target each within . Though they dealt no damage to living matter, they struck inanimate and undead matter with the force of a disintegrate spell, effectively taking a bite out of a  cube.

Were there not enough room for the column to rise to its full height, a single head was created, but its bite could damage a volume spanning twice the usual area in each dimension.

Components
The spell called for verbal and somatic components, but also required a series of material components, including a piece of ferrous material, a stone, and a shard, spar, or long rod of glass.

History
The elves attacking Mythanthar's tower in  employed this spell, amongst others.

## Gallaway Trade Depot

The Gallaway Trade Depot shop in Targos was a part of the prosperous Gallaway Trading Coster that operated in the Northwest Faerûn as early as the late 13 century DR.

Location
The Gallaway Trade Depot stood atop a cliff in Targos, overlooking the town's docks and warehouses that belonged to the Gallaway Trading Coster. Its next door neighbor was the Weeping Widow Inn.

Interior

The Trade Depot in Targos was a sizable single-floor building with its interior filled with shelves, displays, crates, barrels, cupboards, and chests. All the filled with somewhat organized goods that the Gallaway Trading Coster had available for sale, from weapons and armor to scrolls and cooking implements. A fanciful wardrobe stood on the opposite side from the main entrance, behind the counter. It was engraved with an image of a rearing lion and a cackling witch.

Services
Gallaway Trading Coster was the main trading organization in the Ten Towns. It owned several warehouses in Targos's dock, and shipping crates found inside and in the Trade Depot bore trade seals of Bremen and Shaengarne Ford. The coaster exported potions of healing from Bryn Shander, weapons from Luskan, furs and pelts from Lonelywood, alcohol, iron rations, 10-foot poles, and ropes from other places.

The depo offered healing supplies such as bandages and cloth for sale. The arsenal available for purchase was varied, from mundane melee and ranged weapons to a selection of unique enchanted items.

History
The Gallaway Trading Coster was operated by the Gallaway family and brought to prominence by merchant Ilmus Gallaway of Easthaven. Over the years, the small caravan business grew to become a trading coster. By the early 14 century DR, Rawleigh Gallaway led the family, inhered from his father, Ilmus. In turn, Rawleigh's daughter Deirdre Gallaway was put in charge of the company's operations in Targos, personally sent there by Ilmus so that the youth could prove herself. This time would become her biggest challenge as the trade was interrupted by attacks of the Legion of the Chimera, and the coster's goods and lumber were confiscated by the Targos Guard to build the palisades and help protect Town. Jemeliah was one of the employees of the coster in Targos who was assigned the advisory job to Deirdre by her father as "one last favor" after serving the family's business for half of his life. Jemeliah did not approve of the town of Targos' actions and thought that the massive debt they racked up with Deirdre would never be paid off. Deirdre had a reputation of being a brash flame-headed whelp, and she received little respect from Lord Ulbrec Dinnesmore's men.

It curved east along the southern edge of the city, passing by a side lane that held Undreir Facilitations and the newer location of the Windlord's Rest, before passing through the Upper City Gate and down a more gentle slope of the city's knoll that didn't require stairs. It finally terminated at an intersection with the Street of the Sorceress in southern Tilverton.

## Garthond Shessair

Garthond Shessair was an archmage, taught by Jhavanter. He was a foe of the Cult of the Dragon in the early 14 century DR, and the father of Shandril Shessair.

Activities
He was an enemy of the Cult of the Dragon.

Relationships
His master in the Art was Jhavanter. He eventually married with Dammasae, and they had a child, Shandril Shessair. This was unknown to Elminster.

History
Garthond and his master Jhavanter fought the Cult of the Dragon numerous times in Sembia and its near regions. Through that time, Garthond dwelt in the Tower Tranquil. He continued to thwart Cult schemes and survive their attacks; in one of his ambushes upon a caravan of the Cult, he rescued Dammasae. The two began to adventure together and eventually married at the temple of Mystra in Baldur's Gate.

Unknown to Garthond, he had been cursed by the mage Erimmator of the Cult, who placed a balhiir in symbiosis with him during a fight, draining his Art. They left for Elturel, where their child was born; they named the girl Shandril. They left Elturel for the East when the child was months old, accompanied by Gorstag, only to find the Cult waiting for them at the Bridge of Fallen Men near Cormyr. Garthond perished there, slaying nine mages and three swordsmen on his own.

Legacy
Dammasae, Shandril and Gorstag survived the ambush, but Dammasae died shortly after. Gorstag was left to rescue Shandril. The tower Tranquil, unknown to Gorstag, had been seized by the Cult.

## Gate of Good Fortune

The Gate of Good Fortune was a temple to Tymora in the city of Mulmaster in the Moonsea in the mid-to-late 14 and late 15 centuries DR.

Location
It stood in the northeast of the city, near the Black Lord's Altar, a temple to first Bane and then Cyric, and opposite the Tower of the Blades. Usilie Rostik's Smoke Shoppe lay halfway between the Gate of Good Fortune and the House Built on Gold, the temple to Waukeen.

Structure
It was the smallest of Mulmaster's temples but large enough. It was violet in color. A plaza lay in front of it.

Interior
Inside, there were two grand chambers. One was the worship chamber; constantly illuminated with a relaxing purple light, it was a place of peaceful contemplation. The other was the casino, in which a myriad of games of chance and gambling were played for "the holy rites of Tymora". The temple was kept clean and fragrant with incense.

The temple also had a relatively safe and clear entrance to the sewers.

Defenses
The temple's sizeable treasury was guarded by four huge clay golems.

Inhabitants
In the late 1350s and 1360s DR, the high priestess of the temple was the Hand of the Lady Naneetha Danchul, who led nine priests and eleven lay followers. One of the priests was Dzurdan Crommarch and a guard there was Chesslyn Onaubra. By 1373 DR, the high priest was Niskal Rinay.

Activities
The temple also operated as a casino. The house winnings were used for maintaining the temple.

If the Tymoran priests learned of a new adventuring company in Mulmaster, they might reach out to them to offer discounts on healing potions and scrolls.

Every two tendays or so, Dzurdan Crommarch rode south to the village of Maskyr's Eye, accompanied by two followers and four men-at-arms, to collect offerings left at the shrine of Tymora there from the local priest Khonduil Ammargath. If he learned that anyone had desecrated the shrine, stolen the offerings, or hurt the old priest, then Dzurdan would search for those responsible.

History
In the, an earthquake struck Mulmaster. Though neighboring buildings collapsed, the Gate of Good Fortune showed its own good fortune by remaining undamaged, so in the aftermath it became a shelter and gathering place for fearful local Mulmasterites. The Helmite paladin Zern Xerkstil and some adventurers consulted the Tymorans for divinations as to the cause. That was soon revealed as four giant badgers and a monk of the Cult of the Black Earth infiltrated from below and attacked priests and civilians alike. The adventurers dealt with this and pursued the cultists into the sewers.

## Gathen Swiftsword

Gathen, known as the Swiftsword and Gathen Swiftsword, was a retired warrior and adventurer in the village and vale of Maskyr's Eye in the Vast in the late 13 or early 14 century DR.

History
Over time, a number of ex-adventurers had retired to the vale, and occasionally they went to the nearby abandoned mage's tower known as Maskyr's Tower to try their luck on its wards, thinking they might find some heretofore undiscovered vulnerability. Yet its wards proved impenetrable, so they left with nothing. Finally, one of them, Gathen Swiftsword, had grown impatient with this over the years and resolved to take a brute-force approach. He rode to Mulmaster and returned with a 30-strong crew of hired 'strongarm' laborers whom he ordered to simply tunnel through the walls with bars, hammers, and picks.It is unknown when these events occur, but given that Khonduil Ammargath is still alive as of the late 1350s DR, it is presumed to be within his lifetime and thus somewhere in the late 13 century to early 14 centuries DR.

After two days of effort, they'd made one breach and almost had a second. Impatient to begin, the Swiftsword donned his old armor and collected his old weapons, and headed inside. He was never seen again. In fact, in an upper level, Gathen encountered a gargoyle-like guardian monster that proceeded to rip his arms and legs off, and was later transformed into a dragon.

## Gaki fever

Gaki fever was a fatal wasting disease inflicted by shikki-gaki on their victims.

Acquisition
Gaki fever was contracted from injuries inflicted by the claws of a shikki-gaki, and every fresh injury was a new opportunity for infection regardless of whether an individual's immune system had managed to fend off the disease previously. Symptoms would begin to manifest within two hours after an infection took root, and possibly as soon as twenty minutes.

Effects
Sufferers of gaki fever experienced delirium, intense fever and chills, exhaustion, and physical wasting. They were unable to think clearly (such as to cast spells), unable to engage in any strenuous physical activity (even moving quickly was impossible), and experienced a steady degradation of their well-being over the course of the following hours. The disease disrupted the body's natural rest and recovery processes, and even caused the sufferer to resist the effects of most healing magic. If they survived, the disease caused sufferers to waste away over the following days as their stamina was depleted.

Treatment
With great luck, a healthy individual could stave off the fatal final stages of gaki fever, in which case the disease would go away after two to five days of infection. However, in general the disease could only be cured by the use of a remove disease spell or similar magic. Only once the disease had been removed in this way could the individual begin to rest or to receive the benefits of other healing spells.

Appendix
References

---

## Gaudle's rod of rings

Gaudle's rod of rings was a magical item used by the Harpers.

Description
Gaudle's rod of rings was a sleek black wooden stick of similar length to a large man's hand. It was commonly carried sheathed inside a boot or attached to a sword scabbard.

Powers
Upon grasping the rod and commanding its activation, it elongated to the size of a typical rod, accompanied by the manifestation of a ghostly human hand at one end. This spectral hand could bear up to one ring on each digit, including the thumb. Remarkably, these rings remained undetectable and unusable when the rod was in its compact, inactive state. The presence or powers of any magical rings, including gem-encrusted ones, could not be discerned when the hand was not visible.

Typically, rods of this nature were acquired without any rings adorning the phantom hand. It fell upon the user to find or create rings and subsequently affix them to the hand.

When wielding Gaudle's rod of rings, the wielder instantly gained mental awareness of the magical powers possessed by the rings worn on the hand. The wielder could voluntarily activate one power from each ring. Additionally, there was no limit to the activation of involuntary powers. The rod-wielder could concurrently wear and utilize up to two magical rings while wielding the rod.

The phantom hand was very strong. A finger was capable of enduring significant amounts of damage before its ring was forcibly removed or severed during battle. However, the rod-wielder retained the ability to remove a ring at will by speaking a secret command word to release it.

In the absence of rings, Gaudle's rod of rings could only be utilized for its most basic functions. The intangible phantom hand lacked the capability to inflict damage, but its touch could identify any magical item or spell, including magical traps, without triggering them. When in contact with a being, it was able to discern the presence and attributes of any enchantments afflicting that individual.

The phantom hand had the ability to emit an eerie undead-like bony gleam or a faint glow, providing luminosity equivalent to faerie fire, with the hue chosen by the rod-wielder.

The hand could adhere to any solid surface, such as a ceiling, wall, or even slick, wet, oily, greasy, or icy surfaces, through touch and command. This feature allowed the wielder to utilize it as a handhold or anchor in situations where such support would typically be absent. The grip remained in effect until the wielder spoke the word of release or until the rod exceeded a load of . This attribute had been employed by Harpers for descending into glacial crevasses using a line, facilitating cargo transfer between ships at sea by utilizing a mast as a makeshift crane (with a pulley affixed to the rod at a high point on the mast), and enabling various daring rescues or acrobatic swinging-on-a-rope feats.

## Game set of Mujibar the Foolish

Game Set of Mujibar the Foolish was a card game from the city of Calimport in the nation of Calimshan, created sometime before 1298 DR.

Description
The game of Mujibar the Foolish was played with a deck of ninety cards, all stamped with numbers 1 through 9 on ten cards each. The rules were simple. Each player rolled two dice and pulled the number of cards from the deck indicated by the number on both dice. The player with the lowest sum from the card numbers won.

History
The game was popularized by a legendary game of cards that took place in the  between a thief named Mujibar, deep in debt to shady criminal organizations, and a wealthy dao merchant named Khan Zohrin-Ka. The djinn offered Mujibar a gamble: a simple card game of chance. If Mujibar won, all of his debts would be paid off. If Zohrin-Ka won, then the rogue owed the djinn his soul.

Khan Zohrin-Ka went first and rolled "2" on the dice. The sum of his cards was "11" from "7" and "4". Mujibar went next and rolled "10." Horrified, he carefully drew ten cards from the deck without looking at the numbers and not showing them to the dao. Yet all the cards he drew were marked with "1", making the total number "10"—less than that of the djinni. The djinn merchant was honor-bound to obey the deal and pay up, but that didn't mean Zohrin-Ka could not kill his opponent first for stacking the deck. Mujibar was swiftly cut down, his pockets were filled with the winning money, and his body was deposited in a dark alley in Calimport.

Three days later, the rogue's body remained in the alley, but the gold was all gone. Instead, the coins were replaced by thousands of empty ale bottles, remnants of a party of legendary proportions, still told by Calimport's drunks well into the late 14 century DR. Poor drunks of Calimshan were hopeful for another Mujibar the Foolish.

The original deck used in the faithful game ended up in a curio collection of Mintassan the Magnificent, a planeswalking mage merchant from Westgate in the .

## Gavmogon

Gavmogon, also known as the angry eye god to some of his lesser minions, was a beholder who had been transformed into a mindwitness following his enslavement to a colony of mind flayers. He menaced the region around Phandalin in the late 15 century DR, even coming to be seen as a threat among the fey and the other beholders in the area.

Description
He had a large, glowing central eye around which danced his four smaller eyes.

Personality
Gavmogon was mentally unstable and driven by a need for revenge against his mind flayer captors, which turned into a wish to destroy all other creatures that would not bend to his will. He was drunk on his own power and prone to gloating.

Possessions
Gavmogon controlled a massive artifact known as the hollow of dominion, an underground chamber that channeled psychic energy to allow the wielder to commune with the minds of everything within  as well as to project psychic thoughts and control over vast distances. He claimed the complex in which the hollow was located as his lair, and from it was able to detect the presence of any intruders and assault their minds with distressing visions of their worst memories. Naturally, Gavmogon was highly protective of his control over the hollow of dominion and especially of its control device, the dominion rod.

Activities
Gavmogon worked to exert his will over the Phandalin region, believing that he had to prove his superiority by annihilating the minds of everything around him. He agitated monsters, spread madness, and caused chaos. His presence could be detected by some creatures in the form of "eyes in the sky".

History
Gavmogon was enslaved by the elder brain of a mind flayer colony based out of a crashed nautiloid in the Sword Mountains, who transformed him into a mindwitness and gave him potent psychic powers. He managed to escape from the mind control in the late 15 century DR upon discovering the hollow of dominion, which held far greater power than the elder brain. He soon began projecting his influence on the region, leading to strange magical occurrences and more aggressive monsters. The magical disturbances he caused had the effect of attracting a coven of hags to the area, intrigued by the mysterious powers that were disturbing the fey in the region.

Gavmogon personally extended his control over monsters of Crooked Forest. At one point while there, he came across and nearly wiped out a tribe of goblins. The survivors came to revere him as a god—their "angry eye god"—even as he abandoned them. The tribe took on the name of the Angry Eye, and performed sacrifices in the name of their god as they awaited his return. He furthermore agitated a group of gnolls in the forest, prompting them to capture travelers to sacrifice to Yeenoghu. During one of his trips to the Forest, we was briefly glimpsed by one of the gnolls' captives, Alana Hallwinter.

About a year after his initial escape from the mind flayers, Gavmogon finally sought his revenge against the mind flayers. He used the power of the hollow of dominion to manipulate the elder brain into performing a ritual that would supposedly repair their downed nautiloid. Instead, the ritual inflicted madness on the mind flayers as well as all other creatures within  of their colony. This had the result of driving out a group of yuan-ti lairing in the mountains, who began conducting human sacrifices at a quarry near the Old Owl Well.

Gavmogon next began to enact his plan to use the hollow of dominion to inflict madness upon the town of Phandalin and the monsters in the surrounding area. Within a few tendays, an explorer named Aladair identified Gavmogon's lair as the source of the madness, and went to investigate. Subsequently hearing of Aladair's disappearance into said lair, Lady Ilsa Wisemantle of Neverwinter convinced the Town Council of Phandalin to send adventurers to help. At the same time, Gavmogon's machinations caught the attention of other beholders in the region, who both feared his power and wanted it for themselves. One of these rival beholders, Varunto, sent their own agent, the elf Faltrand, into Gavmogon's lair to intercept Aladair and defeat Gavmogon.

## Gatajima

Gatajima was an island located in Kozakura.

Description
Gatajima, under the nominal control of Kanshu, was an island characterized by its windswept terrain and notable absence of harbors. Encircled by steep cliffs and perilous rocks, it posed a challenging obstacle for anyone attempting to access its shores. Only experienced sailors well-versed in navigating the surrounding waters could navigate past the rocky surf to reach its hidden shores.

Inhabitants
Precisely due to its formidable nature, Gatajima has become a favored sanctuary for those seeking refuge and seclusion. Over time, Gatajima has served as a hiding place for wako pirates and other outlaws, who have occasionally stored their ill-gotten treasures within its confines.

## Gathlarue

Gathlarue (also known as Gathlarue the Wonder Wizard) was a Zhentarim wizard of the mid-14 century DR. She kept her gender a secret from the rest of the Zhentarim.

Description
She sounded older than her apprentices.

Personality
Gathlarue was dissatisfied by her apprentices.

Possessions
She carried a ring of teleportation, until Tespril, her apprentice, filched it. At the time of her death, she wore a dark robe. She also wore an amulet that protected her against all poisons, almost certainly a periapt of proof against poison.

She also bore a scrying element, a round sphere of crystal, through which she saw not just events but also thoughts to which she responded, strongly implying it was almost certainly a crystal ball with detect thoughts.

Gathlarue carried a number of enchanted rings that glowed blue when activated; those were destroyed soon after. She controlled gargoyles through such rings.

Abilities
She could cloak herself in a male disguise with her Art. She also knew of a blood spell that had bound her apprentices to her, letting her sift their thoughts at will.

Amongst other spells, she knew one to make herself capable of flight, almost certainly fly, one to arrest her falls, almost certainly feather fall, and a spell that created barriers capable of blocking spellfire which her apprentice Tespril knew as a "forcewall", almost certainly a wall of force. One of her spells allowed her to control darkenbeasts, almost certainly create darkenbeast. She could send visions to distant people.

Relationships
Her apprentices were Tespril and Mairara, the latter being also the eldest. She was intimate with both, and in fact often engaged in sadomasochistic play.

She avoided Karkul Memrimmon, and was aware of the goings-on around the search for spellfire. Amongst other things, she was aware of the deceits of Ghaubhan Szaurr.

History
At some point, Gathlarue had explored Myth Drannor, where she'd found a portal atop a roofless tower leading to a hidden redoubt, where she'd discovered numerous spellbooks, likely including wall of force. In, she joined the hunt for spellfire after participating in the meeting in which Sarhthor and Manshoon explained the hunt for Shandril Shessair.

Throughout the hunt, she scried on Shandril constantly. Later on, she sent a darkenbeast after Shandril's party. She later employed magic to send visions to Ramath, while scrying on Ghaubhan Szaurr, whom she hoped to send after Shandril.

She decided to attack finally at Irondrake Rock. During the attack, she sent waves of gargoyles before attacking herself; her attack was short-lived, and unsuccessful. She protected herself from the spellfire with a wall of force or similar spell, but by attacking the cliff atop her, Shandril was able to bury her under rock.

Rumors & Legends
Per Fzoul Chembryl, Gathlarue wanted the Brotherhood ruled by women instead of men, and he was aware and dismissive of her disguised gender.

## Gaea

Gaea was a greater power of earth, a progenitor of the greater titans, and grandmother of the first Olympians. She was also, according to some, an embodiment of every Prime Material world, though the latter was more likely just Olympian boasting. There was no evidence of direct worship for her on the Prime Material world of Toril.There were no known worshipers of Gaea in the Realms. However, Gaea is mentioned in the opening pages of the Living City module No Time to Lose. For this reason, Gaea is documented in this article.

Description
In her true form, Gaea is the earth itself. Her avatar took the form of a beautiful, mature and buxom woman.

Personalities
Over time some mischaracterized Gaea as some sort of schemer that jealously craved retaining her deific position and strength in light of the Olympian pantheon's rise to power. In actuality she was a very concerned and loving motherly figure. She was also rather aloof, rarely ever meddling in the affairs of mortals, and had a liking for hideous monsters.

Abilities
Avatars of Gaea could call upon the spheres of all, animal, charm, elemental, healing, plant, and weather for her spells. In addition, any being that looked uninvited upon her avatar could be petrified or blinded for one to ten days.

Overall, the power that Gaea had at her disposal far outweighed that possessed by the Olympian deity Zeus.

Possessions
The avatars of Gaea wielded a black sickle that had the power to instantly slay those struck by it.

Activities
Gaea sometimes provided aid to mortals in dire need, in return for sacrifices of fruits and animals being made to her. However, she never provided aid in fighting against hideous monsters. She potentially would even provide aid to such monsters brought to her attention.

History
According to the Olympians' understanding of how the multiverse came to be, as documented in their the tome the Great Theogony, before everything began there was nothing but an indescribable roiling mass of chaos. From this chaos arose various aspects of nature, such as the planes of Arborea and Tarterus, and a handful of primeval gods that included Gaea and Eros. While the Olympians' story of creation was steeped in their own egocentric biases, it was true that Gaea was an incredibly old deity.

Gaea would then create and marry the primeval god Uranus. With their union they created a race of beings known as the Greater Titans, who by some accounts numbered eleven or twelve in total. Gaea then went on to birth more creations. Some were fair in appearance like the Titans, whilst others were far more monstrous, such as the cyclopses and the hecatoncheires. Uranus, proving to be a cruel and jealous man, would keep all of her more monstrous children locked up as he hated to look upon them. Some accounts told of them being locked up inside her earthen depths, causing Gaea pain, while others told of them being locked up in Tarterus. Either way, with time Gaea grew sickened over the imprisoned state of her children.

Gaea convinced one of her children, the greater titan Cronus, to overthrow his father and thus avenge her mistreatment. Cronus did so, severely wounding Uranus. The blood from his wounds fell upon Gaea, fertilizing her once more, causing the creation of the Furies and the gigantes.  The wounded Uranus then fled from the battle to the farthest reaches of the multiverse, leaving his final fate unclear.

Cronus then married the Titan Rhea and assumed command of the Greater Titans, going back on his word to Gaea that he would free her monstrous brood. Furious, Gaea laid a curse upon Cronus, proclaiming that one day his own children would usurp him in turn. Eventually Gaea's curse came to pass and Cronus was usurped by his own son Zeus, who alongside his siblings led the way for the creation of the Olympian pantheon.

The newly formed Olympian pantheon's first act following this was the casting away of the majority of the Greater Titans into Tarterus. Not wishing for any of her children to be imprisoned, Gaea tried to send the gigantes and a hundred-headed dragon against the Olympians to liberate and avenge the Titans. Her creations brought the Olympians to their knees, but the new generation of gods ultimately held up against them. Despite this and some other early attempts to avenge or liberate her children, Gaea remained an ally of the Olympian pantheon.

In the month of Hammer, in the, a group of adventurers from Ravens Bluff found themselves transported to the plane of Tarterus after defending the githzerai fortress of Tah'Darr from a tanar'ri incursion. At the urgings of the fallen paladin Elendil, the adventurers sought out Gaea's child Cronus for a way back to Toril.

Worshipers
Unlike other deities, Gaea did not need proper direct worshipers, which led some to claim that she wasn't a proper deity. Instead, it was believed that the constant life and movement on the Prime Material worlds was equivalent. Nor did she need require temples or sacrifices, only desiring that those on the Prime treated their worlds with love and respect.

Whenever Gaea had proper priests, they were always druids. Weapons commonly wielded by them included clubs, quarterstaves, sickles, and various agricultural implements. They had access to spells from the spheres of all, animal, divination, elemental, healing, plant, and weather. Among the spells they always had access to were cure disease and divination. They also were liable to know the spells control weather and control winds.

The druids of Gaea oversaw tasks appropriate to her portfolio, such as animal husbandry, planting, and ministering aid to the sick. They also often served as oracles for their communities.
Notable Worshipers
In terms of the philosophical planar factions of the multiverse, Gaea was worshiped by some members of the Sign of One.

Galdegut's Boneyard was a glue factory, workshop and shop in Lannort in the 15 century DR.

Services
The Boneyard sourced a wide variety of bones thanks to Morluth Galdegut's network of shady suppliers. These bones were worked into various alchemical ingredients, melted into glue, or sold wholesale for other purposes. It was linked by merchants' map to Samril's Fine Blades in Secomber, who used bone (particularly the more durable bones of perytons, owlbears, dire wolves, and fire lizards) to carve pommels for weapons such longswords.

Gandrel's Aspiration possessed a +1 enchantment that guided the bolts towards their targets and increased their damage. The crossbow also possessed magic that made it especially effective against monstrosities.

Gandrel named his heavy crossbow after the incident he witnessed at seven years old – a vampire spawn being burned at the stake. In the, the Gur carried this crossbow while hunting for the vampire spawn named Astarion Ancunín across the Western Heartlands and the city of Baldur's Gate.

## Gaheid Slickjaw

Gaheid Slickjaw was a dragonborn commoner, displaced by The Absolute, and as a refugee, sought shelter in Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Gaheid was a large green-scaled dragonborn with axe-shaped horns and flaming red eyes. He wore a plain frilly-necked tunic and simple tan-colored breeches.

History
In the, an army assembled by the Cult of the Absolute moved from the Moonrise Towers in Chionthar Valley to menace the city of Baldur's Gate, displacing countless refugees in the process along the way. Gaheid Slickjaw and his beloved Hunkers Boolean found their way to the Rivington district outside the city walls, but, like all other refugees, they were refused entrance. The two men had to live in the tent city, only able to gain entrance with proper paperwork. Gaheid thought, somewhat sheepishly, that picking fights with the Flaming Fist mercenaries was a bad idea, to what his partner called him the "most cowardly dragonborn in the Realms." Despite calling Gaheid a blockhead, Hunkers claimed to feel love for the man.

## Gambiton

Gambiton was a small village in southern Amn.

Description
The tiny village was home to two businesses of note that faced each other across the intersection. The Gambitavern was its tavern, and the Gambitinn was the inn.

Geography
The village lay at the intersection of the Imnescourse Trail and the South Road, a few miles south of the King's Arch bridge across the River Esmel that led to Esmeltaran. To the east near Lake Esmel was the Five Silvers, a ring of ancient standing stones.

History
Gambiton gained its name from "Imnel's Gambit," as it was the site where Prince Imnel Torlath I divided his forces in two during the Ogre Wars in the . The nearby Vale of the Wailing Women was named for the lamentations as his soldiers marched away, and Imnescar was named for the eventual battle that he won after splitting his army.

The village was destroyed in the, in the opening of the Sothillisian War. An army of Skullgnasher hill giants and Spearbiter goblins poured from the Small Teeth and quickly overran the nearby Hillfort Ishla, and then razed Gambiton the following day on Eleasis 8 on the way to Esmeltaran. The surviving population fled to the west and north.

## Gauntlet of Shar

The Gauntlet of Shar was an enormous underground temple dedicated to the dark goddess Shar and a training ground for the Sharran faithful. It was the birthplace of the elite Sharran force, the Dark Justiciars.

Location
The Gauntlet of Shar lay in an Underdark cave beneath the Thorm Mausoleum, which was located in northwest corner of the town of Reithwin. The entrance was hidden behind a mural describing the tragic life and loss of Ketheric Thorm. A platform elevator in the shape of a Sharran disk would take the visitors from the surface world to the dark depths.

An alternative entrance to Gauntlet of Shar was using the platform elevator in the Grymforge. However, this passage was sealed and destroyed when orthon Yurgir came to slaughter the dark justiciars.

Function
The Gauntlet of Shar was the sacred crucible for determining the most faithful Sharrans, those who wanted to fully dedicate themselves to Shar and become her implements in inflicting the goddess's wrath upon enemies and in restoring her eternal darkness.

The initiates would undergo several trials to test their strength, wisdom, and flexibility. With each success, they would be granted an umbral orb to unlock the path forward. When they were deemed worthy in every trial, the initiate would use the orb to unlock the way to the inner chamber of the temple. In there, they would make their final preparations and make the most glorious sacrifice in Shar's name to ultimately become a Dark Justiciar.

History
The temple was built at Ketheric Thorm's command when he served as Shar's Chosen during the 14 century DR. After Ketheric turned his faith from Selûne to Shar, he and his advisor Balthazar deceived Ketheric's former colleague Aylin, the immortal daughter of Selûne, into going into the Shadowfell. They trapped Aylin with infernal chains and a soul cage. Aylin became a ritual sacrifice to Shar for the completion of faithful Sharran transformation into Dark Justiciars. The Gauntlet of Shar was built up around Aylin's prison and Aylin was killed and revived to be killed again for years.

While the Dark Justiciars were fighting an alliance of Harpers and Emerald Grove druids on the surface world in the later 14 century DR, an orthon mercenary Yurgir came to the temple to kill the remaining Dark Justiciars by the terms of a contract. All the inhabitants of the Gauntlet of Shar were slaughtered except a Dark Justiciar named Lyrthindor, who escaped with help from Raphael. Yurgir couldn't find the last Dark Justiciar, nor could he leave the temple without breaking the contract. So Yurgir made his lair within the empty temple. After that, the temple was abandoned and fell into ruin for the next century with the later coming of the shadow curse .

In the, Balthazar was tasked by Ketheric Thorm with retrieving the Nightsong to secure Ketheric's immortality while Ketheric served as the general of the Cult of the Absolute. Balthazar and his undead servants managed to get into the long-abandoned temple, but they were prevented from venturing forward by Dark Justiciar ghosts manifested from the Shadowfell.

Inhabitants
 Yurgir, an orthon who was contracted by Raphael to slaughter every Dark Justiciar, but was trapped within the temple by the terms of the unfinished contract.
 Nessa, Yurgir's displacer beast.
 Balthazar, the necromancer advisor of Ketheric Thorm who came to retrieve the Nightsong.
 Flesh, Balthazar's meat golem.
 Lyrthindor, the last Dark Justiciar who escaped from Yurgir by turning into a swarm of rats.
 Nightsong, the immortal daughter of Selûne who was trapped in shadowfell and made as a sacriface for dark justiciars.

Galem Meats was a smuggler who worked with the infamous Guild in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Meats was a mountainous grey-skinned half-orc with long wavy lush hair. He was dressed in a simple breastplate atop bright green cloth, and carried a massive two-handed axe.

History
In the, Jezzy worked as a muscle for a gruff hin smuggler named Big Huido, transporting weapons, drugs, and firewine into Baldur's Gate. The shipments were well defended by Galem and the rest of the crew – all were released into the hands of the members of the Guild only. ## Gargantua (aberration)

A gargantua was an aberration born from giants that grew to greater than normal size.

Description
Gargantuas had a large and muscular body, hairless purplish skin, horns, and bony protrusions around their body. They were much taller than even other giants, reaching heights of at least .

Biology
A gargantua was born as a normal child of giant parents, looking like its corresponding type of giant, but once it reached adulthood it mutated. It gained a purplish complexion, grew horns and bony protrusions around its body, and quickly grew significantly bigger.

Personality
The majority of gargantuas did not feel at home in giant communities and started looking for homes that were large enough to fit their extremely large frames. Some of them only wanted peace and solitude, but others turned aggressive against those they thought feared them.

Abilities
Gargantuas had an aura that could cause others within 30 feet (9 meters) of them to see the gargantua as a manifestation of their greatest fears, inflicting psychic damage and frightening them. They could also incapacitate creatures they could see within  of them with a curse. Furthermore, they could teleport and mimic spells just by observation.

Their bodies were resistant to force and psychic damage and they had superb senses, being able to see in the dark and having great perception in general.

They could not be frightened.

Combat
Gargantuas usually fought off attackers by throwing rocks or crushing them with their body. But they also utilized their various strange abilities, like their aura, curse, or spell mimicry.

History
It was not known for certain what caused normal giants to mutate into gargantuas; rather, there were only stories. Some said that their origin was linked to a pact between an ancient enclave and an entity of the Far Realm. Others claimed that the giant god Karontor, a banished son of Annam, created gargantuas as revenge for his imprisonment. Still other stories laid the blame for gargantuas with Annam and claimed that he was disgusted by his descendants and wanted to destroy them somehow.

Appendix
Background
The word "gargantua" originates in Gargantua and Pantagruel, a series of 15-century French fantasy novels, where the two title characters are a pair of giants.

References

---

Gamalon's gem was a large ersatz eye carved out of emerald.

The gem eye had as a permanent infravision spell active, allowing Gamalon Idogyr, its owner, to see even in the deepest darkness of the Underdark. Additionally, Gamalon's gem had a permanent detect magic spell weaved into its enchantments.

## Gallissoniere

Gallissoniere was a Netherese thief who plied his trade in Eileanar shortly before its destruction in .

History
While in the enclave, Gallissoniere discovered the criminal history of Cadogen, an Underchief of Constabulary Branch Three who had lived a long life in the shadows. He proceeded to blackmail Cadogen for three months, demanding information on the constabulary's operations and movements, until finally Cadogen had enough. He killed Gallissoniere, and then claimed that the rogue had drawn a knife and attempted to fight his way to freedom during a routine arrest.

## Garb of the Land and Sky

The Garb of the Land and Sky was a unique set of monk robes that one belonged to the famous monk Kushigo sometime before the late 15 century DR.

Powers
Even though this garb could be worn by anyone, only monks could utilize all of its enchantments. Its mountainous poise enchantment allowed the monk receive effects of the blade ward spell when they used the patient defense technique. The breezeswift speed enchantment allowed the monk to briefly boost their protection and attack after using the step of the wind technique. Once the monk used the step of the wind technique, their defense received a magical boost similar to that of a +1 enchantment and their blows were guided to the targets, dealing additional force damage.

History
Once, long before the late 15 century DR, the Garb of the Land and Sky was worn by Kushigo, a warrior who gave up his heavy shell for the freedom of movement after suffering a great loss. In the, the Garb of the Land and Sky could be found in Danthelon's Dancing Axe's inventory, among mostly mundane items in stock.

## Gariskar Dauntinghorn

Gariskar Dauntinghorn was a nobleman of Cormyr and a head of the Dauntinghorn family in the late 15 century DR.

Description
The handsome, smooth-looking man had a fierce smile. His hair was dark; he gave the impression of an old sea lion.

Relationships
He was the patriarch of the Dauntinghorn family, and a rival noble to Marlin Stormserpent.

History
In Mirtul of the, Marlin Stormserpent paid the artist Jharakphred to follow two people, Lord Gariskar Dauntinghorn and Draskos Crownsilver and then paint a portrait of them. The artist shadowed them for days so closely that he had to be beat off by their retinue with cudgels. Marlin then ordered his two blueflame ghosts, Relve Langral and Treth Halonter, to learn the men's likenesses from their portraits to better identify and kill those men. In the eve of the Council of the Dragon, blueflame ghosts murdered Seszgar.

## Gambit armor

Gambit armor was a type of enchanted armor designed for those unafraid to risk their lives in battle.

Description
Cloth or leather armor could be enchanted as gambit armor.

Powers
While this armor provided enhanced protection, its true power lay in granting the wearer advantages in battle when their life was in particular danger. This gave them a greater change of scoring a deadly blow immediately after receiving one, and once per day, the wearer could become more dangerous at the expense of exposing themselves to greater risk of injury.

Notable Owners
As of the, an Executor of the Warlock Knights of Vaasa had enhanced their leather armor into gambit armor. This Executor was part of the team responsible for investigating the rejuvenated Castle Perilous.

Gau was a Netherese shipwright who worked at Gallery Steamers in Eileanar until its destruction in .

Activities
Gau served Stuckley as a work crew manager for the construction of flying ships used in Netheril. She worked alongside fellow managers Fourcrow, Apulee, and Bruck.

## Gaskur

Gaskur was a bodyguard and servant to Marlin Stormserpent in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He was quite nondescript.

Personality
He was not one to second-guess his master.

Possessions
Gaskur was not known to own any property.

Activities
Gaskur did all that Marlin required of him, and being a bodyguard was only a small part of his service.

Relationships
He worked for Marlin Stormserpent. He was no stranger to kicks from nobles who found him not obsequious enough. As far as Marlin knew, he had no kin nor family in Suzail, but actually he had nieces, and these nieces had cats.

History
By, Gaskur had served Marlin Stormserpent for six or seven seasons.

Gaskur accompanied Marlin Stormserpent in his foray to Dragonskull Chamber of Castle Obarskyr, where he stayed out the door while Marlin experimented with the control of Blueflame ghosts using the Wyverntongue Chalice and Flying Blade. Later, he ushered in the lords Delasko Sornstern, Broryn Windstag, and Kathkote Dawntard when they were looking to see Marlin Stormserpent after their murder of the officer of the Crown, Delnor, while drunken in the Dragonriders Club. Gaskur was attending to Marlin right up until the moment where the latter was approached by the two Blueflame Ghosts, Relve Langral and Treth Halonter, after completing their appointed task: slaying Seszgar Huntcrown and fetching his head. Marlin resolved to tell Gaskur to clean up after them, and to rake the corpse's bones out of the furnace.

Unknwon to anyone, the evil archmage Manshoon had resolved to slay Gaskur for one of his intrigues: he was going to send Talane to murder him. Gaskur's body was brought to Marlin afterwards; according to the watch, he was murdered on a roof, and the word "talane" scrawled on that same roof with his blood before he was kicked onto the floor.

## Gaerond

Gaerond of the Scars was an adventurer leading the Bloodshields Band in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He had sword scars, making him fearsome.

Personality
While professional, he was not above greed.

Abilities
He was very quick.

Possessions
He carried a longsword with peace-strings, and at least two longsarks.

Activities
He was a veteran adventurer.

Relationships
The Bloodshields Band numbered six, including him, Rornagar Breakblade, Flamdar, Malkym and Lylar.

History
Gaerond led the Bloodshields Band into Shadowdale in, on commission from a rich Sembian who had paid a large amount upfront, looking for Elminster Aumar, the ancient archmage. Gaerond came first upon the Old Skull Inn, where he was able to commission the youngster Thal, actually a doppelganger, to lead him to Elminster. Thal took them to a cave, where Gaerond met a doppelganger pretending to be Elminster; when they revealed they actually intended to decapitate Elminster and carry his head as a trophy to Sembia, a number of hidden doppelgangers fired a volley of crossbows into the band, slaying them almost on the spot. Gaerond was the last to die, though not by a great difference.

## Garendor

Garendor was a sage of Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, alongside Loagranboydar, Orkrash, Wyshbryn, and Argrant, Garendor had been given the task of investigating the secret records of noble family trees. The War Wizard Glathra Barcantle sought to make use of their knowledge to find out who had sent a blueflame ghost against the Crown. However, when Glathra and Vainrence went to meet with them, they found them to have been reduced to piles of ash, with magic still hanging about their remains.

With Loagranboydar presumed to be named after Eric L. Boyd and Orkrash after George Krashos (as suggested by Krashos here, it is almost certain that Wyshbryn may also be named after a person from the real world known to Ed Greenwood.

## Garlow

Garlow was a lifelong servant and an attendant who worked for the owner of a large townhome in Baldur's Gate and eventually inherited it sometime before the late 15 century DR.

History
Garlow spent most of his life working in his master's home in the Lower City. His lifelong service was rewarded with the master died. Garlow inherited the entire mansion. Despite the inheritance, Garlow found himself consumed by the regret of a life wasted. He penned a journal he simply called "Inklings" and made a bucket list for his own retirement: to climb a tree, have a truly exceptional sandwich, spend day just reading, visit the City of Splendors, Waterdeep, get a massage, go for a swim in the River Chionthar, buy a new coat, and lastly, get a cat. Later, Garlow decided to leave the manse behind and leave for Calimshan to spend the rest of his life traveling from there. He left a kind letter to whoever would claim "Old Garlow's Place," asking them to take care of the place that was his entire life. However, the place was not cared for; it remained abandoned in the  when it was inspected by Ardent Low, the Council of Four Liaison, who proclaimed it to be in gross violation of the city by-laws 38a-39c and condemned in accordance with by-law 42a. Liaison Low left a notice on Garlow's home, and recommended the owner to contact the local Builder's Guild representative in regards of immediate repairs.

## Garmor

Garmor was a novice duergar member of the Cult of the Absolute during the late 15 century DR.

Description
Garmor had gray skin and long white dreadlocks that framed his bearded face adorned with white barbarian pain. He did not wear armor and charged into battle bare-chested, wielding a greataxe.

History
In the, Garmor accompanied slaver Gekh Coal and his duergar med to a decrepit coastal village in the Underdark that once was a deep gnome colony. On the behest of the Absolute, duergar held the ruined village to be used as a skiff port to transport goods and slaves to the Grymforge. With the events of the Absolute crisis unfolding, Garmor and his fellow duergar could have faced several fates; they could've been confronted by and slain by the Absolute Adversaries as they sought passage to the Grymforge or as a favor to an aspiring myconid sovereign Glut.

## Garbler

Garblers were formless aberrations that wielded Truespeak.

Description
These masses of flesh and teeth babbled incoherently constantly.

Personality
Garblers were nominally sapient. They spoke but did not understand or communicate in any coherent language. Even telepathy did not allow communication with anything beneath their madness.

The creature lunged towards any flesh it discovered, out to feed.

Abilities
The creature moved in a disjointed manner, dragging itself along with its flowing jaws. As it gibbered, it occasionally uttered something in truespeak in its "reversed" form, using them to hamper foes rather than bolster allies. Garblers employed six such utterances: defensive edge, morale boost, temporal twist, greater speed of the zephyr, spell rebirth, and preternatural clarity.

Despite the fact that a garbler did not understand any languages, it could effortlessly "counterspeak" or reverse any Truespeak utterance used in its vicinity. It had an uncanny, instinctive understanding of what was good and what wasn't, and could not be counted on to reverse a positive utterance on itself nor a negative utterance targetting an enemy. Bizarrely, a garbler found Truespeak to be no challenge whatsoever, and had no chance of erring in its utterances.

Being eyeless, it was unaffected by gaze attacks.

Society
They were solitary.

History
As of, the Red Wizards of Thay had furnished themselves with a few garblers captured recently from the Underdark. This contact with truename magic made it out to be a threat, as far as they could tell.

References

## Gasper Throaks

Gasper Throaks was a Fist of Bane who served the Cult of the Absolute in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Gasper was a dark-skinned human man with curly brown hair. He wore a set of cultist chain mails adorned with Bane's black hand and was armed with a simple shield.

History
In the, the Cult of Bane joined forces with the Cult of the Absolute, led by the Chosen of Bane, Lord Enver Gortash. Gasper Throaks was recruited into the cult by Black Gauntlet Tamia Holzt, who had an affair with Gasper's mother, Clovia Throaks, unbeknownst to Blarson, his father. Assigned the title of Fist of Bane, Gasper Throaks was placed in the Steel Watch Foundry, working under Tamia Holzt, guarding its Gondian slaves in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate.

## Garrison (Surcross)

The Garrison of Surcross in Thay was a building that housed the living portion of the city's Thayan soldiers as of the late 15 century DR.

Structure
It was the second-most fortified building in the city (after the Tombstone) and was a long hall of reinforced, heavy stone.

Interior
The building contained many inner passages, rooms, and chambers comprising the communal barracks. Each room of the barracks could house dozens of soldiers, who were segregated by race (into humans, gnolls, orcs, half-orcs, and "other") but not segregated by gender. These barracks were simple, often including not more than a footlocker and a cot for each soldier. The exception was a single, opulent room that served as the barracks for a contingent of warlocks known as the Sunmasters, who had wardrobes, luxurious mattresses, thick carpets, and fine food and wine.

The structure included a main hall, at the end of which were a series of teleportation circles that allowed the troops to instantly travel to the Tombstone, the Gates of Bone, or any of the five Fingers of Szass Tam.

Defenses
Undead guards watched the garrison's entrances and did not allow entry to anyone who did not have a pass or a uniform, either of a Thayan soldier or a Red Wizard.

References

## Gar's Road

The Gar's Road was a road somewhere in Faerûn during the mid–8 century DR.

Geography
The Gar's Road stretched east and west from its intersection with the Larrauden in a vale it shared with Buckralam's Starn, a small village. To the west of Buckralam's Starn, the Gar's Road touched the eastern ridges of Howling Ghost Wood, going over the top of Hurtle Tor, and leading to the Shrine of the Sheaf.

## Galadorna

Galadorna, also styled The Unicorn Kingdom, was a kingdom in the Wizards' Reach until the mid–8 century DR.

Geography
Galadorna was in the Wizards' Reach, centered around Nethrar; it did not extend all the way to Laothkund.

Government
A king reigned in Galadorna. There were a number of barons under him; among those barons were Tholone, Hothal, Maethor, Feldrin, and Belundrar. Lesser lords existed, such as Limmator.

Trade
Galadorna had mines of iron and silver.

Defenses
Each baron of Galadorna had significant retinues and all of them contributed to the kingdom's defense.

History
During the reign of King Baerimgrim, the self-styled Lord Tholone, one of the barons, had been ignoring court decrees since approximately .

One bright summer day in the, about a year after the king was gravely injured by the green dragon Arlavaunta, King Baeringrim held a meeting with his barons to determine the successor; they chose Tholone to be the next king. However, both men were slain by Nadrathen, a Chosen of Mystra, who crowned himself king in their place. After his fellow Chosen, Elminster, stopped him, the Banite sorceress Dasumia slew Nadrathen and took over as Queen of Galadorna.

Under her reign, Galadorna became rife with feuding mages and was chaotic yet wealthy.

The Unicorn Kingdom was ultimately destroyed by the Lords of Laothkund, within a tenday of the Queen's disappearance, very likely within the year.

## Galleass

The galleass was a large type of ship that resembled a cross between a galley and a galleon.

Description
Galleasses were extremely large ships that had both sails and rowers, with the intent to have the seaworthiness and strength of a galleon and the speed and agility of a galley. Despite these ambitions, the galley-derived form meant that it lacked the stability for true oceanic travel.

When under sail, the galleass was much more sluggish than other sailships, but its rowers could bring it up to a respectable speed that was even more pronounced in adverse wind conditions that would cripple other ships.

A galleass could make good use of its speed and maneuverability with its ship ram, and would easily fit ballistae on the deck.

Usage
Galleasses had very large cargo holds, and so worked best as merchant ships along coastal waters, but could still be used as warships when equipped with large marine detachments.

Notable Galleasses
 The Wave Wyvern, owned by the Seasilver noble family of Marsember, later captained by Falrinn Greatorm.
 The Gleason, owned by the Dhostar family of Sembia.

Appendix
Appearances

## Gallaher Brym

Gallaher Brym was a scribe refugee from the Rivington neighborhood of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, as the armies of the Absolute ravaged the Western Heartlands, scribe Gallaher Brym and his family were among countless refugees seeking entrance to Baldur's Gate. The family was not allowed passage through the Wyrm's Crossing, forcing them to remain in Rivington's refugee tent city. Desperate for admission into the closed-off city, Gallaher penned a letter, sent via the Sword Coast Couriers, addressed to Lord Patriar Haverford Gist. The scribe offered his professional services for a mere nominal fee and eternal gratitude if Haverford, renowned for his charitability, would send to word to the Flaming Fists to allow the family enter the city. It was unknown whether Lord Gist responded to the request or even if the letter was ever delivered.

Appearances

## Garden gnome

Garden gnomes, also known as lawn gnomes or stone gnomes, were ornamental statues of gnomes meant for decorating gardens. They were known in such far-flung places as the Blade Kingdoms, Waterdeep, and Loudwater.

Description
These ornaments were typically small with pointy hats, and had a tendency to be painted rather garishly.

History
Sometime in the 15 century DR, a Waterdhavian citizen came into the offices of the Watchful Order of Magists and Protectors to absolve him of a bet he had made at a gambling table to a spellcaster that he believed to be Khelben Arunsun, despite popular consensus among Waterdhavians being that the Blackstaff was dead. The man claimed that the victor of the bet demanded he provide one of two impossible things by the next night: either an elemental of chocolate syrup or "a mermaid succubus who's human from the waist down". If he failed to provide either, the man would be polymorphed into either a lawn gnome or a myconid fountain forced to hold a selection of vegetables. The exasperated magist on staff that day, Maerlus the Magnificent, assured the man that the Watchful Order could provide him a short-term semblance of either in return for the appropriate fees.

Reputation

Gnomes had mixed opinions on these strange ornaments, with some feeling that they were either secretly an act of reverence or mockery by humans.

Notable Garden Gnomes
The gardens of the Enchanter's Ecstasy inn in Loudwater in the 1360s DR were "infested" with stone gnomes, as part of its cutesy decorations and kitsch décor.

In the Thief's Knot hedge maze in Waterdeep's City of the Dead, there was a stone gnome carved to resemble the Master Thief's most respected rival, a thiefcatcher. Once stern of face beneath a pointy hat, weathering had rendered it more amused than anything. A trap triggering alarm bells was cunningly concealed behind it, and Sophraea Carver and Gustin Bone nearly set it off in the .

## Gahrzig

Gahrzig was a wemic chief and mercenary who worked for Pristoleph, a powerful figure in the city of Innarlith, on several occasions during the 14 century DR.

History
Senator Pristoleph began building an army at Firesteap Citadel in the Firesteap Mountains south of Innarlith in the, with the intention of invading the city-state and overthrowing Ransar Salatis. He hired a large number of wemic mercenaries from the Shaar as part of his army, including Gahrzig and his tribe. He paid them with magic weapons.

Gahrzig was present in the final assault on the Palace of Many Spires in the, which ended when Salatis's own bodyguards murdered him and power was handed over to Pristoleph. Gahrzig and his wemics were present with Pristoleph outside the door of Salatis's bedchamber when the bodyguards presented the body of their former ruler to Pristoleph.

In the, a faction of senators united together against Ransar Pristoleph and Gahrzig with his wemics again fought for Pristoleph in the civil war that ensued. He was wounded by acid from a black firedrake during street fighting, but survived.

## Gart Stonenose

Gart Stonenose was a dwarven shopkeeper of Freedale in the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
The dwarf had only one eye.

Personality
Gart was very grumpy, except among friends.

Abilities
He was a capable warrior, but had some difficulties due to the loss of an eye.

Possessions
As the proprietor of Freedale's General Store, he had just about one of everything.

Activities
He was the owner and shopkeeper of the general store in Freedale. He doubled prices for people he disliked.

Relationships
He was friends with local adventurer Delvar Ironfist.

Once an adventurer, Gart had to retire from adventuring after losing an eye. He had since become the shopkeeper for the General Store in Freedale.

Gart Stonenose is adapted from First Quest, a similar book to Introduction to Advanced Dungeons & Dragons except set in Karameikos. In Quest, his functions were combined with those of a moneychanger.
