## Garius

Garius was a rogue active in the Moonshae Isles during the late 3 century DR.

Personality
As a thief, Garius had no issue with harming others for the sake of profit, but he despised those who acted for the sake of wanton cruelty.

History
Garius traveled the world before joining the Doomed Legion to serve Prince Ketheryll at the Castle of Skulls. Although bound to serve and protect the Prince, Garius instead came to despise his evil master.

According to local legends on Gwynneth, Garius managed to flee the Castle of Skulls on the night that it became cursed—the Summer Solstice in the . However, the truth was that he was caught by Ketheryll, who used his magic to transform the rogue into a cheap tin knife.

In the following centuries, the Castle would become a haunted place in which an undead Ketheryll dwelt. To lure victims to his lair, Ketheryll used his magic to cast illusions over Garius to make the cheap knife appear to be made of platinum and gems and then planted him in the nearby forest to attract treasure-hunters. Garius also gained the ability to point in the direction of Ketheryll and the Castle as a way to ensure that adventurers would find their way there.

In the, the halfling adventurer Pawldo and his companion Stefanik sought out the castle after the latter found the Garius knife. The pair dared to delve into the castle seeking treasure, but encountered the monstrous Ketheryll. Garius helped them to fight him off by using its pointing power to help Pawldo determine which of multiple copies was the real Ketheryll.

Appendix
References

---

## Gannis

Gannis was a human conman active throughout the taverns of the Sword Coast in the late 15 century DR. He adopted different surnames and backstories in each city to which he traveled.

History
In Baldur's Gate, Gannis posed as a merchant named Gannis Tath seeking to raise funds for an expedition to Chult. He sold false gemstones to patrons at the Low Lantern, and claimed to introduce his marks to a Chultan tradition that bound them in an unbreakable trust. By the time that he was outed as an imposter by Artus Cimber, he had already fled Baldur's Gate. A vengeful kenku assassin soon pursued him.

In Waterdeep, he adopted the identity of Gannis Frostmantle and claimed to be an emissary of the Tuigan working to undermine demonic forces. He used this story to woo Iokaste Daliano, the proprietor of the Pink Flumph, and conned her out of a great deal of coin. She ultimately realized that he was lying when he failed to recognize traditional foods of the Endless Wastes, and she and her companion Wishes drove him out of town using illusions to make him believe that the gods of the Tuigan were angry with him. He fled from there to Luskan.

In Luskan, he claimed to be Gannis Redthumb, a fugitive nobleman from Damara fleeing from persecution. He settled in at One-Eyed Jax and convinced drunk patrons to buy the seeds of peaches and strawberries that he claimed to be magically hardy and resistant to winter snow. However, it was here that the kenku assassin from Baldur's Gate caught up to him, and he wound up dead in the mouth of the River Mirar.

Garick was one of the druids that joined his cause, either of his own accord or the powerful will that Halsaime exerted, and guarded Thorn Hollow with his companion dire bears until they were overcome by Harper agents sent from Eveningstar by Kirinel Zay.

He used his exceptional skill in tracking to locate the secretive Thorngap druidic refuge, leading the Harpers to Thorn Hollow and then the Druid's Deep, allowing them to neutralize the rogue druid Halsaime. Gamorst was the leader of the Tribunal of Evernight as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
As a member of the Tribunal, he was a powerful ghoul and a follower of Orcus.

Personality
Gamorst was succinct and impatient. He immediately demanded tribute in exchange for the Tribunal's time, and he allowed for no interference in their decisions.

Abilities
He had the ability to destroy even a lich with a mere point of his finger.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, the undead residents of Evernight held Gamorst responsible for the unpopular rule that allowed the Tribunal to extend their protection to mortal visitors to the city.

When the lich Zarifrax attempted to slay a mortal to whom Gamorst had extended the Tribunal's protection, Gamorst swiftly destroyed him and claimed his phylactery.

## Gast

Professor Gast was a demilich hobgoblin who taught at the Dungeon Academy.

Description
As a demilich, Gast appeared as nothing more than the skull of a hobgoblin surrounded by magical blue fire. He wore spectacles and had a wheezing voice.

While the Dungeon Academy banned all forms of undead from the premises, an exception was made for Professor Gast.

Personality
Gast refused to admit that he had ever died, believing himself to still be a living hobgoblin. He had a fear of mirrors. He was very intelligent, but often talked for long periods in a dull tone. He was also self-important.

Abilities
Before becoming a demilich, Gast had been a wizard. As a demilich, he had the power to hover in the air and invisibly manipulate objects.

Activities
Gast taught History of Horrible Humans at the Dungeon Academy. He taught classes by lecturing and used sticks of chalk to mark a chalkboard. Gast would send students to detention for interrupting. He offered special essays for extra credit. He was known to conduct experiments.

Relationships
Nearly every student at the Dungeon Academy detested Gast, and it was likely that some of the staff did as well.

History
As a living hobgoblin, Gast once traveled from the Dungeon Academy on a journey to Waterdeep. The purpose of this trip was to educate others on how dangerous adventurers could be. He was caught and killed by humans, and returned to the Dungeon Academy as a demilich.

One winter, Zellidora Stormclash interrupted one of Gast's History of Horrible Humans classes, during a lecture on her biological mother Allidora Steelstrike and the Steelstrike Family. He sent her to detention. Her friend, Snabla, insulted Gast and was also sent to detention.

Some time later, during the Tourney of Terror, Gast searched dormitories of the Dungeon Academy for the escaped necromancer Carrion. He did not notice the Danger Club and Tavian Truescale who were sneaking around.

## Garthin Muirtree

Garthin Muirtree was a tradelord of Neverwinter in the mid-to-late 14 century DR, who was mysteriously murdered in .

Personality
He was liked well.

Activities
As a tradelord of Neverwinter, he represented its interests in negotiations with other organizations.

Relationships
He was a foe of Dauphran Alskyte and friends with Draevin Flarwood.

History
Garthin Muirtree was a well-traveled man. He met with Alustriel Silverhand only twice, the first time being years before . He had also met with the mage Janthasarde Ilbright in previous trips to the city.

On that year, he was installed at the Red Griffon Room during his visit to Silverymoon at the request of Auvrarn Labraster, who claimed to fear a "sneaking magic". The tradelord met with a number of people on his final day there, in sequence being Auvrarn himself, Dauphran Alskyte, Draevin Flarwood, and Janthasarde Ilbright. He was to meet with Oscalar Maerbree as well. After all the visits, he was found murdered in that room by the steward Rorild.

Murder Investigation
The Red Griffon Room housed a dead magic zone, making it almost certain no magic was used in his killing. However, when Alustriel was checking the dead magic zone for tampering, she realized that some kinds of magic, those that came from and affected the user's own body and nothing else, such as the glow of her own silver fire, would still work. Interrogating Oscalar Maerbree, she learned the meet between Maerbree and Muirtree did not materialize, as Oscalar received a note stating the man had taken ill. Instead, someone else, disguised in Oscalar's likeness was led by them to and from the room; only by checking all of her palace's internal couriers did she learn that Oscalar told the truth about the note. This led her to question all the other men who met with the dead man.

When talking to Auvrarn Labraster, the man suddenly snapped in outrage, asking how he could've savaged the man so thoroughly with nothing but his own pocket knife. However, the man swore up and down he had not killed him and that the Muirtree had been alive last they spoke, and that any honest magical examination would reveal as much. This instantly fingered him as an accomplice, as she'd never told him the man had been savaged thoroughly.

## Garrett the Bold

Garrett the Bold was a charismatic womanizer and an ambitious treasure hunter from the city of Westgate in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Garrett was a slender and handsome half-elf. He was a dashing man with bronze skin, red hair, a quick smile, and trustworthy, bright eyes.

Abilities
Garrett was a talented rogue, profecient in lockpicking and scouting.

Possessions
Garrett used an enchanted +1 short sword.

History
In the, during one of his escapades, Garrett learned of a treasure hidden within a lost temple underneath Westgate. The Hidden House, a temple to Leira, a dead power, was accessible via a certain magical ritual, which was uncovered by Garrett. He was an ambitious man and smart enough to know that lost temples tended to house forgotten dangers. Garrett needed muscle to accompany him into the Hidden House. He approached a small group of adventurers led by a recent arrival to Westgate on a quest opposing the nefarious Night Masks, offering them partnership in pillaging the lost temple. Garrett offered the adventurers all the treasure they would find, except for a single item, which he would not reveal until the item was found. He was searching for a great Leiran artifact known as the Cloak of Mysteries.

The group followed Garrett to a statue in front of a nobleman's mansion, where the ritual was performed—a recitation of a prayer to Leira, which transported them into the underground temple. Within the dark, abandoned sacred ground, the group was met by a priestly ghost who demanded that the intruders begone. The adventurers ignored her warnings, Garrett noticing that the ghost of a slain priestess did not look particularly sad, and continued into the magical labyrinth of teleporting chambers.

When they reached the inner chambers, it became obvious that the ghost was just an illusion created by the oni mage Skyrim to scare intruders away from his new lair. The adventurers and Garrett battled the oni and his ogre and orc henchmen, defeating them and driving the monsters away from the ruined temple. They collected the Cloak of Mysteries from the oni's possessions. However, it was not known if the adventurers slew Garrett over the artifact, allowed the man to claim his bounty, or left the temple and the artifact behind in peace.

## Garadic

Garadic was a servant of the wizard Ilhundyl in the 3 century DR.

Description
He wore the guise of a tall, handsome man, but was actually a scaly creature with large fangs, wings, warts, and a shambling manner. Other disguises of his' included a grizzled, older man.

Personality
He spoke very little.

Abilities
He was able to shed his disguises, though in a gruesome manner.

Relationships
He served the mage Ilhundyl.

History
Garadic was serving Ilhundyl in,Elmara finds the book in Marpenoth of 233, but she has been with the Brave Blades since the previous snow season, which means before the start of 233. Thus, she cannot have started before the year 232. Furthermore, she abandons Hastarl on or after 229, and spends two seasons (years) with the elf Braer, meaning it cannot be before 231. This article assumes she tarries longer between Braer and Ilhundyl than between Ilhundyl and the Brave Blades, thus making it more likely she met with him in 232. when Ilhundyl was visited at the Castle of Sorcery by Elmara, a priestess of Mystra looking to become a wizard. After she caught his interest, he called on Garadic to bring her to his presence. There, Ilhundyl bade her deliver Ondil's Book of Spells to Mystra's Dance, a shrine. He admitted to Garadic afterwards that he meant for her to die, for he'd spotted the most potential for magic out of any individual he'd ever met.

Once she acquired the book on the  and travelled to Mystra's Dance, Garadic was there, disguised as the boat ferryman that took her there. He was also present later back in the Castle when Ilhundyl attempted to read Ondil's Book of Spells. The emptiness of the book and the taunts of the Magister Dorgon Heamiiolothtar whom he farspoke afterwards drove him into a rage, in which he hurled spells that slew Garadic.

## Gaspar-shen

Gaspar-shen was a watersoul genasi adventurer, sailor, and swordsman active in the Moonshae Isles as of the late 15 century DR.

Description
Gasper-shen was shorter than the average genasi. He wore very little clothing, favoring simply a pair of eel-skin breeches. Across the blue skin of his exposed torso and bald head, lines of energy glowed with different colors depending on his emotional state. He had small lips and seemed incapable of smiling.

His voice was eerie and airless, sounding calm but with a high-pitched, almost whistling quality.

Personality

He had a pure and emotional connection to elemental water, and embodied its chaotic and free associative nature. He had no interest in directing events to better suit him, and he took joy in moments of quiet, even if they occurred while he was in danger. He had no desire to make or build things, although the ingredients and efforts that went into baking desserts and pastries fascinated him. He spoke frequently about exotic desserts, some real and others clearly imagined. While he sometimes expressed an interest in trying such delicacies, in truth he had no interest in the desserts themselves and never ate them. Instead, his interest was metaphorical, and he discussed desserts as a way to express his feelings about current events and predicaments. He rarely spoke directly or literally except in dire situations, and it was often hard to tell if he was joking. He had no patience for music.

When aboard a ship, Gasper-shen spent his time laying on his stomach on the bow, feeling the spray and sensing the depth and movement of the water beneath him. He slept very little.{{Cite digital book/The Rose of Sarifal/Kindle||7}

Abilities

He was vigilant, and quick to warn his comrades of any potential or incoming dangers. He was a skilled combatant, and wielded a scimitar that burned with cold fire in one hand and a shortsword in the other. He favored targeting his opponents' torsos and bellies.

Relationships
He was a member of the crew of the Sphinx, alongside his captain, Lukas, as well as Suka, the Savage, Marikke, and Kip. He had a long history with Lukas in particular, although the human sometimes struggled to relate to and understand the genasi. Gasper-shen respected and deferred to his captain, although not always without sarcasm (especially when Lukas led them to take idiotic risks).

History

Gasper-shen hailed from the deserts of Calimshan, but his soul was said to be from Abeir and he regarded the Elemental Chaos as his true place of birth. At some point, he made his way to the Moonshae Isles, an environment that was much more welcoming to his water-oriented body and spirit. There, he partnered with Lukas and the two of them worked for Amnian interests transporting cargo aboard the Sphinx between Snowdown and Alaron.

In the late 1470s DR, Lukas accepted a job to escort Lord Aldon Kendrick to the ruins of Caer Corwell in Sarifal, which turned into a quest to kill Lady Amaranth, a princess of the leShay rulers of Sarifal, who led a group of lycanthropes on the island of Moray. The crew of the Sphinx was soon scattered, with Suka being imprisoned in Caer Corwell and the Savage, Marikke, and Kip being captured by the Black Blood tribe after the Sphinx was destroyed by fire nagas. Meanwhile, Gasper-shen had rescued Lukas from drowning in the shipwreck, and so the pair of them set out to find and rescue their abducted companions. Instead, they got lost, and after following Lukas's lead in ambushing a raiding party of orcs, Gasper-shen received a serious head wound. He only survived thanks to the intervention of Lady Amaranth and her followers, who brought him to Caer Moray to recover. After he was stabilized, he spent much of his time in a bathtub of salt water as he recovered, but was forced into action once again when the Black Blood laid siege to Caer Moray.

Following the Battle of Caer Moray, Gasper-shen traveled with Lukas and Amaranth back to Sarifal, and came to learn the fates of their lost crewmates along the way. Gasper-shen remained by Lukas and Amaranth's side as they were captured by drow cultists of Araushnee in the Underdark. They escaped that predicament with Araushnee's own blessing, and as they did so, she granted Gasper-shen a vision of his future, standing on a rocky beach far away. Pondering this vision, he asked Amaranth (who had her own reckoning with the goddess) what food she desired most, and when she answered "ortolans"—a bird that was killed and prepared in a grotesque manner—he determined that he did not trust her around Lukas. He bade her to leave him, which was incidentally what Araushnee wanted of her as well.

Gasper-shen and Lukas were ultimately reunited with Suka just as Amaranth left them to negotiate their safe escape from the armies of the leShay Prince Araithe. The three of them then left Sarifal by boat.

## Gazzeth

Gazzeth, or simply the ruined orc city, was a long-abandoned ruin of a city along the coast of the Moonsea.The name Gazzeth is only seen on the map provided in The Moonsea boxset and is deduced by its proximity to Thentia and Seawave (also on said map).

Geography
It lay between the city of Thentia and the Seawave ruins, and between the Ruined Trail and the shore of the Moonsea.

History
The name 'Gazzeth' was lost to history and, given the legend surrounding it, few in the Moonsea were eager to find it. By the mid-1300s DR, the ruins of Gazzeth were known simply as "ruined orc city".

Orcs from the ruined city destroyed Seawave, a nearby colony of Thentia.

Inhabitants
Giant forms of many kinds of vermin infested the ruins as of the late 1360s DR, which kept people away. At one point, it was also inhabited by orcs.While called "the ruined orc city" and "the orcs of the ruined orc city" destroyed Seawave, the source does not clarify if this was an orc-built city or merely a ruined city occupied by orcs at one point and merely named for them. It may be one of the many human-built cities destroyed by the Dark Alliance, an informal collection of evil humanoids and no relation to the Dark Alliance videogame franchise, spearheaded by the land of Thar, mentioned on page 3 of the Reference Guide, or it may be a far older construction.

Rumors & Legends
A legend formed around the city's forgotten name, claiming that if the name of the city was ever discovered, then its long-dead inhabitants would rise, gather into an army, and march on Thentia.

There were rumors in Thentia that dragons and beholders might be establishing lairs in some of the ruined cities to the east, which would include Gazzeth.

## Galath (magelord)

Galath was an apprentice of the Magelords of Athalantar in the early 3 century DR.

Personality
In the opinion of the senior magelords, Galath was over-eager, inept, and tiresome.

Abilities
Many of the magelords privately harbored scornful views of Galath's abilities. Briost Elthauryn, for one, wondered if he could put on a tabard the right way, knew how to unroll a scroll, or even how to open a spellbook.

Relationships
Galath served the Magelords of Athalantar. Briost Elthauryn, a magelord, had had his patience tested to its limits by Galath.

History
Galath was present in a moot of the Magelords, attended also by Briost, Undarl Dragonrider, Nasarn, and Alarashan Tlor in the . During the meeting, the elimination of Elmara, a priestess of Mystra who was secretly the prince Elminster Aumar, was discussed, as Prince Gartos had been sent after her. Though Galath was eager to learn and to serve, he said many things that came off as unwise, obvious, or impractical to the other magelords; only Undarl gave him credit for enthusiasm.

## Garbh Lludd

Garbh Lludd was the leader of a large tribe of firbolgs active in The Highlands of the island of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles as of the late 15 century DR.

Personality
She was a celebrated hunter, boasting considerable skill as a tracker as well as a relentlessness in pursuing her prey in battle. Her favored prey included basilisks, battlebriars, and dire bulettes.

Abilities
Garbh Lludd was a Mistress of the Wild Hunt, and accordingly wielded mighty primal magic that made her a frightful leader among fey of all kinds. She was able to fly (albeit clumsily) and could wield the power of the moon to weaken and inflict grievous wounds against her prey. She could command Wild Hunt hounds.

Activities
She was the mightiest warrior of her tribe. Under her leadership, they were nomadic and hunted along the westward slopes of The Highlands down toward the Shrouded Lands.

Possessions
She fought with a spear that she was able to summon back to herself if thrown. She carried a light shield and wore a stag-antlered helmet. In battle, she rode in a signature chariot pulled by a great elk.

## Gaston Wyrst

Gaston Wyrst was a Shadovar wizard of Returned Netheril who was deployed to Beckside Castle in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Gaston had pale skin, short brown hair, and wore a dark brown and gray robe with the symbol of Shar.

Personality
Though more restrained and professional than his companion Keldan, Gaston was not above arrogantly monologuing at his enemies.

Abilities

Gaston was a shadow wizard and could summon umbral rats en-mass. He could also cast chain lightning, crushing despair, delayed blast fireball, meteor swarm, polar ray, and thunderstroke.

History

When the Shadovar pursued their stolen Nether Scroll along the High Road to Beckside Castle, Gaston and Keldan Shadeskein led the recovery efforts there. They infiltrated past the Weathered Dawn with an escort of mercenaries into the other parts of the ruins, with Gaston going directly for the Scroll while Keldan erected a defensive shield. Finding the Scroll piece in the ruined throne room, Gaston was heavily delayed by a magical barrier that surrounded it, which he struggled to break through. By the time he managed to retrieve the piece, the Harpers had arrived and killed Keldan, then faced him in battle. Though he summoned many umbral rats to his aid, Gaston fell and the Scroll went to the Harpers.

## Gatekeepers

The Gatekeepers was a druidic sect of orcs and half-orcs in the world of Eberron. It was devoted to protecting the world from planar invasions, aberrations, fiends, and other threats that emerged from uncontrolled portals.

History

In ages long past, the Gatekeepers were founded to protect Eberron from planar threats, eventually thwarting a cataclysmic invasion from Xoriat. In the years that followed the druids, rangers, and barbarians of the Gatekeepers continued to guard against invaders and hunted down those that slipped through.

During the mid-1480s DR of Faerûn, the Lords of Dust managed to break into the Spinner's Prison and weakened the bonds on the Spinner of Shadows. This led to Lolth herself breaking open a Rift Between Worlds from the Demonweb Pits and allowing the Spinner to escape. Worst of all, the rift continued to grow and threatened to destroy the world of Eberron in the process.

The Gatekeepers joined the Church of the Silver Flame and the Twelve at the scene of the disaster, and lent their efforts to containing the portal. Despite this, the portal continued to grow, and Tavnar Kronnak made the decision to send a small team of heroes through to explore the other side and hopefully find a solution. Most fortunately the heroes met Elminster and found their way to Eveningstar, taking part in a greater struggle against Lolth and saving the Weave in the process. With the guidance of Valana they also stabilized the rift, preventing it from growing any larger and thus saving Eberron as well.

## Garwyn Thane

Swordcaptain Garwyn Thane was a Purple Dragon of Cormyr, who was stationed in the town of Eveningstar during the invasion from Sschindylryn in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Garwyn was a large human man with brown eyes, black hair, and dark skin. He wore a suit of chain armor and a simple white surcoat, and carried a large longsword.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Garwyn worked under Constal Adamor Thellik in pushing back the drow from the area and defending the town. When the second Battle for Eveningstar was imminent, he organized the Cormyrean side, using his Purple Dragons, War Wizards, the Evenor militia, adventurers, and even Elminster as assets. In particular he had the use of Ana Brabener, a young local girl with an unexpected magical talent, which he used to shore up the defenses further. Garwyn proved to be a skilled commander, as he correctly deduced that the drow would not use the Darkening as cover but instead attack at night. The battle went off as well as anyone could hope, defeating the drow and ending their siege of the village.

## Garbs of the cubi

The garbs of the cubi was a set of enchanted light armor with its origins tied to Avernus, the first layer of the Nine Hells of Baator, created sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

Description
The garbs of the cubi were clothes of red and orange ornamentation that remained spotless and undamaged, while retaining a stench of fire and brimstone.

Powers
When the wearer of these clothes managed to deal devastating damage to their enemy with a single attack, the enchantments pulled the target towards the wearer for the duration of five seconds. If a creature was unaffected or resisted the effect, they also suffered trauma that caused them bleeding wounds.

History
In the late 15 century DR, the garbs of the cubi were worn by Jachinthe the Vain, a succubus that dwelt in Avernus. The creature may have been slain by a group of adventurers from Neverwinter and the garbs taken back to the Prime Material plane during the .
